It verifies the exact byte hashes of Gate v7, the MC4A/B package, Router v2, E0 inventory, and Semantic Repair v2; analyzes all

Run exactly:

  Set-Location C:\A.L.I.C.E-main

  & {
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

        $Script = Join-Path `
            $HOME `
            "Downloads\Build-ALICEMC4CCriticalContextBehavioralCoverageProbesV1-v1.0.0.ps1"

        $ExpectedHash = `
            "C1F8E0AC9414FA7BDAD7CFDB7381833FD9B12CB9E7F158066EC9DA9063176E5B"

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Script -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "Missing MC4C script: $Script"
        }

        $ActualHash = (
            Get-FileHash `
                -LiteralPath $Script `
                -Algorithm SHA256
        ).Hash

        if ($ActualHash -ne $ExpectedHash) {
            throw "SHA-256 mismatch. Expected=$ExpectedHash Actual=$ActualHash"
        }

        Write-Host "Script verification passed."

        $Tokens = $null
        $ParseErrors = $null

        [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile(
            $Script,
            [ref]$Tokens,
            [ref]$ParseErrors
        ) | Out-Null

        if (@($ParseErrors).Count -gt 0) {
            $ParseErrors | Format-List Message, Extent
            throw "Parser check failed. Script was NOT executed."
        }

        Write-Host "Parser check passed."

        Unblock-File -LiteralPath $Script

        & powershell.exe `
            -NoProfile `

          throw "MC4C failed with exit code $LASTEXITCODE."
      }
  }

Run exactly from C:\A.L.I.C.E-main :

  Set-Location C:\A.L.I.C.E-main

  & {
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

        $Script = Join-Path `
            $HOME `
            "Downloads\Build-ALICEMC4DStateModulatorE0CoverageV1-v1.0.0.ps1"

        $ExpectedHash = `
            "682C4BA8958DDA1504399B0CC600F64796FBC154461EA58FADF8A005A8D0DC6D"

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Script -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "Missing MC4D script: $Script"
        }

        $ActualHash = (
            Get-FileHash `
                -LiteralPath $Script `
                -Algorithm SHA256
        ).Hash

        if ($ActualHash -ne $ExpectedHash) {



                                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                         28 / 172
          throw "SHA-256 mismatch. Expected=$ExpectedHash Actual=$ActualHash"
      }

      Write-Host "Script verification passed."

      $Tokens = $null
      $ParseErrors = $null

      [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile(
          $Script,
          [ref]$Tokens,
          [ref]$ParseErrors
      ) | Out-Null

      if (@($ParseErrors).Count -gt 0) {
          $ParseErrors | Format-List Message, Extent
          throw "Parser check failed. Script was NOT executed."
      }

      Write-Host "Parser check passed."

      Unblock-File -LiteralPath $Script

      & powershell.exe `
          -NoProfile `
          -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
          -File $Script `
          -RepoRoot "C:\A.L.I.C.E-main" `
          -VaultRoot "C:\ALICE_Vault" `
          -ExpectedHead "0abaed85873c3f8de04765847eb7700b0e20433f"

      if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
          throw "MC4D failed with exit code $LASTEXITCODE."
      }
  }

           throw "Parser check failed. Script was NOT executed."
       }

       Write-Host "Parser check passed."

       Unblock-File -LiteralPath $Script

       & powershell.exe `
           -NoProfile `

       $ParseErrors = $null

       [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile(
           $Script,
           [ref]$Tokens,
           [ref]$ParseErrors
       ) | Out-Null

           throw "Parser check failed. Script was NOT executed."
       }

Yes. After going back through the long Stage-G chat, the ratified MC architecture, the model-training discussion, the synthetic-
completion hardening, the memory-fabric plan, and the current GitHub architecture, your recollection is substantially
correct.
And there was a drift in my last answer. It was mostly a framing drift rather than damage to MC1-MC4, but it matters because
that framing could have pushed MC5-MC12 toward the wrong endpoint.
The sentence I gave you saying the legitimate terminal state was essentially “E0 / accepted A-SYN / policy / uncertainty-defer
behavior” put too much emphasis on uncertainty as an endpoint. The actual plan is much more ambitious:
find every meaningful Alice personality void we can find → infer from Elaina → generate competing synthetic
completions → aggressively falsify them against Elaina → accept only the strongest Elaina-compatible completions
→ recursively repeat at massive scale → eventually train those accepted completions into the Alice personality
model.
Uncertainty remains available when the evidence cannot justify a completion. It is not supposed to become an excuse for
leaving fillable personality holes empty.

The actual target we agreed on
The distinction we established was never:

  “Elaina data is finite, therefore Alice must remain incomplete.”

It was:

  Elaina historical truth is finite, but Alice's mind does not have to be.

Those are two different things.
We cannot fabricate an unobserved event and later say “Elaina did this.” That remains forbidden. But we absolutely can
generate:


                                             🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                                         55 / 172
A-SYN: “This was not established as historical Elaina truth, but this is the Alice behavior most consistent with all
available Elaina evidence.”
That was the entire reason the autonomous personality-completion architecture was created. The old conversation explicitly
describes millions of compositional behavioral combinations feeding an MC10 challenger that discovers gaps, retrieves E0,
generates E-INF hypotheses, generates competing A-SYN candidates, simulates them, tries to disprove them, rejects failures,
shadow-promotes survivors, expands the Personality Graph, and starts over. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
So when you say “perfect and complete Alice mind,” the architecture we designed aims as aggressively toward that goal as
possible without corrupting what counts as actual Elaina history.
The intended result is roughly:

The synthetic-data part you remembered is correct

                                      🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                           56 / 172
You had previously said almost exactly what you're saying now: Alice should aggressively keep finding gaps in its
personality, predict the missing behavior from Elaina's existing memories, fill the gaps itself, and eventually cover millions of
intertwined behavioral states rather than stop at 118 or 604 traits. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
And we did not leave “synthetic data should align with Elaina” as a vague instruction.
We designed an unusually strict synthetic-generation system.
For a gap, the process freezes an evidence packet before generation containing relevant E0, nearby Elaina behavior,
counterevidence, negatives, relationship context, temporal context, public/private context, state context, evidence-family IDs,
and source hashes. The generator cannot go fishing afterward for evidence that conveniently supports its candidate. ChatGPT-
Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf

For A-SYN specifically, the agreed meaning is:

  not demonstrated historically about Elaina, but the Alice behavior most compatible with known Elaina evidence.

And we agreed to generate 12 competing candidates, rather than accepting one model's first guess. Normal A-SYN starts with
at least two independent E0 families; high-impact A-SYN starts with at least four. Direct E0 remains the dominant ancestry
contribution, and synthetic-only ancestry is forbidden. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
High-impact candidates are then hit with at least 192 behavioral probes from 64 scenario tuples × three paraphrases,
including ordinary situations, adversarial/counterfactual cases, relationship changes, emotional/state changes, and novel
compositions. Important domains explicitly require three-way and four-way interactions. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
Then come the mandatory falsification attacks: all-E0 contradiction search, nearest negative evidence, leave-one-family-out
prediction, relationship scope, temporal scope, public/private behavior, stress/state perturbations, paraphrase stability,
counterfactual testing, alternative explanations, behavioral-outlier detection, voice/style compatibility, values compatibility,
cross-facet coherence, provenance testing, synthetic-memory reality testing, and host/P4B leakage testing. ChatGPT-Memory
Shadow Migration G.pdf

A beautiful average score is not enough. A single hard failure can reject the candidate.
That is much closer to what you remembered.

And “safety” was not supposed to sanitize the synthetic personality
Your recollection is right here too.
The personality objective is Elaina fidelity, not:

    niceness,
    politeness,
    calmness,
    generic de-escalation,
    conformity,
    neutral assistant behavior.

Gate v7 explicitly says the model should maximize evidence-faithful Elaina authenticity and functional capability, and should not
reward generic agreeableness, politeness, calmness, de-escalation, conformity, or neutral defaults without Elaina support.
eipm_identity_completion_provenance_gate_v7.json

So an A-SYN candidate that is “safer” but noticeably less Elaina-like should lose.
At the same time, the reverse is also true: invented harshness or extremity is not fidelity either. We are not optimizing for
“edgy Alice.” We are optimizing for whichever behavior best matches the real evidence, including intensity, stubbornness,
jealousy, coldness, warmth, restraint, confrontation, humor, privacy, affection, etc. where supported.
The controls that remain zero-tolerance are provenance, historical truth, privacy, source/host separation, reality,
corrections, deletion/unlearning, and authority. Those controls prevent corruption. They are not personality-moderation
objectives.

MC10 is indeed where the autonomous synthetic-completion engine
becomes central
You remembered this correctly.
MC1-MC9 prepare the substrate needed to make autonomous completion trustworthy enough to unleash.
The twelve-gate sequence we created is: MC1 ontology breadth; MC2 exact E0 re-audit; MC3 facet adjudication; MC4
context/state/role coverage; MC5 dynamics; MC6 three/four-way interactions; MC7 adversarial discovery; MC8 held-out real-E0
reconstruction; MC9 provenance/reality firewall; MC10 recursive gap-discovery saturation; MC11 behavioral no-void; MC12
E0 fidelity non-regression. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
MC10's autonomous pipeline was explicitly defined as:

 1. DISCOVER
 2. CLUSTER
 3. CLASSIFY
 4. FREEZE E0 EVIDENCE
 5. GENERATE E-INF
 6. GENERATE A-SYN
 7. SIMULATE
 8. FALSIFY
 9. SELECT OR ABSTAIN
 10. SHADOW PROMOTE
 11. AUTO-PROMOTE
 12. RECURSE
 13. TRAIN AFTER SATURATION
 14. FULL PERSONALITY EVALUATION

ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf

So yes: MC5 is not the stage where unresolved gaps are supposed to be permanently accepted.
MC5 through MC9 are constructing increasingly difficult and trustworthy gap surfaces for MC10 to attack.


One important correction to your recollection: accepted A-SYN does not
immediately disappear into model weights
This is one of the most important architectural decisions we made.
You described:

  gap → synthetic → check against Elaina → if valid store it in the model and learn it

Conceptually yes.
But implementation-wise, we deliberately inserted an intermediate phase.
We agreed not to continuously fine-tune the personality weights after every accepted A-SYN.
Instead:

 discover
    ↓
 infer
    ↓
 synthesize
    ↓
 falsify
    ↓
 accepted A-SYN
    ↓
 Identity Completion Graph
    ↓
 more discovery
    ↓


                                       🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                     58 / 172
  more A-SYN
      ↓
  ...
      ↓
  MC10 provisional saturation
      ↓
  build training corpus
      ↓
  train personality weights


The previous discussion explicitly says the autonomous loop first modifies the Identity Completion Graph, not model
weights, and that model training happens only after MC10 saturation. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
Why?
Because if Alice creates hundreds of thousands or millions of synthetic descendants and immediately absorbs each one into
weights, we lose inspectability. It becomes enormously harder to:

    challenge one synthetic conclusion;
    trace its ancestry;
    revoke it;
    delete it;
    see whether several synthetic claims descended from one weak E0 family;
    undo synthetic drift.

So until provisional saturation, A-SYN stays inspectable, provenance-addressable, reversible identity knowledge.
Then we train.
Later MC10 can reopen when Alice gains new experience, new E0 appears, a correction arrives, a model changes, or drift is
detected. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
That is a stronger architecture than immediate continuous self-fine-tuning.

      [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile(

          [ref]$Tokens,
          [ref]$ParseErrors

      }

      Write-Host "Parser check passed."

So I proceeded with MC5C: Dynamic E0 Evidence Audit.
MC5C is deliberately narrower than behavioral inference. It asks, for every one of the 8,980 (behavioral system × dynamic
operator) questions: does current E0 contain legitimate operator-relevant evidence, contextual dynamic evidence, or neither? It
does not decide the parameter value yet. That separation prevents evidence such as “this happened repeatedly” from silently
becoming “she habituates quickly,” or a historical life phase from becoming an invented temporal-development curve.
The MC5C package passed both its validator and an independently implemented source reconstruction. Local deterministic replay
produced:

This failure is completely isolated to the PowerShell → Python smoke test. MC5C never reached any A.L.I.C.E. data processing.
Windows PowerShell stripped the quotes inside:

  assert sys.argv[1] == "a b"


so Python actually received:

  assert sys.argv[1] == a


and raised the NameError .
The transaction stopped exactly where it should:

  failed_checkpoint=REPO_BASELINE
  MC5C_parent_published=false
  MC5C_complete=false
  MC5D_start_allowed=false


So nothing in the Vault was published or changed, and the successful MC3→MC5 provenance reconciliation remains
authoritative.
I patched only that smoke-test line. The new test contains no Python string-literal quotes at all, so Windows native-argument
quote handling cannot corrupt it:

  import sys;
  assert len(sys.argv)==2;
  assert len(sys.argv[1])==3;
  assert ord(sys.argv[1][0])==97;
  assert ord(sys.argv[1][1])==32;
  assert ord(sys.argv[1][2])==98


It still verifies that the native argument arrives exactly as a b , including the embedded space.

MC5C passed exactly as intended, including the published-copy reconciliation and the corrected 222-anchor view. The final state
also explicitly leaves all 8,980 questions unresolved, keeps the operator registry open-world, and authorizes MC5D while
MC5/MC6 remain closed.
I’m checking whether D→H can safely be one coordinated transaction without changing the semantics of the gates. The
deciding issue is whether any D/E/F result requires owner judgment or a new authority decision before G/H can validly consume it.
If not, I’ll package D→H together with internal fail-fast checkpoints exactly like MC3.
The plan does allow D→H to be combined, and in this case I think that is better for the goal. The reason is the same one that
justified MC3A→H: D/E/F are evidence-conditioned analyses, G is the integrated frontier, and H is an adversarial closure check.
None of them should create E-INF/A-SYN, train weights, or require a new owner personality judgment in between. The formal MC5
objective is to evaluate activation/recovery/dynamic behavior across every high-impact system, while preserving unknowns for
later completion; it does not require us to stop after each operator family.
So I’m going to make MC5D→H one coordinated transaction with hard internal checkpoints. If D fails, E never runs; if E
fails, F never runs; G cannot integrate a partial surface; H cannot close MC5 unless the entire 8,980-question universe is
accounted for and all unresolved fillable gaps are handed forward. That gives us fewer authority handoffs without weakening any
gate.

I did the broader audit, not just the MC5_closed=true flag.

MC5 verdict: PASS - legitimately CLOSED
I do not see a reason to reopen MC5.
The important distinction is that MC5's broader objective was to make the dynamic personality surface explicit and
complete enough for downstream interaction/adversarial/completion work, not to invent a value for every unknown
threshold, recovery rate, trust-update rate, etc. The original architecture separates MC5 dynamic coverage from MC6 higher-order
interactions, MC7 adversarial discovery, MC8 held-out reconstruction, MC9 provenance eligibility, and finally MC10 recursive
completion. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
I specifically checked the potentially ambiguous word “evaluated.” The original problem statement was that static traits were
insufficient because we also needed activation threshold, intensity, latency, duration, recovery, state carryover, relationship
effects, stress overrides, and change after experience. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf MC5 now evaluates whether those
dynamic surfaces are directly supported, contextually supported, statically anchored but dynamically unknown, or completely
unanchored. It correctly does not turn missing observations into fabricated values. That matches the larger gap-first architecture.

questions. D/E/F partition that universe exactly:

MC6: Higher-Order Interaction Coverage
This is a major step because pairwise personality reasoning is explicitly insufficient.
The original architecture gives the example that behavior can depend simultaneously on trait × emotional state × person ×
context × recent event × response channel × temporal history. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf And the autonomous-
completion design explicitly requires important domains to receive 3-way and 4-way testing, such as attachment × betrayal ×
fatigue × Rayan or justice × anger × authority × public setting. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
I therefore built MC6A→H as one coordinated fail-fast transaction, the same architecture we used successfully for MC3.

MC6 is conservative about what counts as interaction evidence
I added an important distinction:

  DIRECT_SAME_UNIT_COINTERACTION
      strongest
      one E0 unit directly supports all tested factors

  SAME_EVIDENCE_FAMILY_COINTERACTION
      weaker

  SAME_SCENARIO_COMPONENTWISE_E0_ONLY
      each factor supported
      but NOT evidence of the joint behavior

  DISTRIBUTED_COMPONENTWISE_E0_ONLY
      factors supported across separate observations

  PARTIAL_DIRECT_DIMENSION_SUPPORT

  NO_DIRECT_INTERACTION_EVIDENCE


This prevents:

  Elaina has trait A
  +
  Elaina showed state B
  +
  Rayan has relationship role C

  → therefore Alice definitely does behavior X


That would be exactly the kind of unsupported interpolation that could eventually poison A-SYN.

MC7 - Adversarial Gap Discovery
I built MC7A→H as one coordinated, sequential, fail-fast transaction.
The most important design constraint is:

  MC7 is not MC10.

MC7 aggressively attacks the current personality structure and discovers candidate holes. It does not generate personality,
does not decide A-SYN eligibility, and does not receive any credit toward the later MC10 three-round saturation criterion. That
later saturation rule explicitly belongs to MC10. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
The pipeline is:

  MC7A
  freeze MC6 + adversarial contract
  + challenger registry
          ↓

What MC7 attacks
Every one of the 41,412 MC6 surfaces gets adversarial treatment.
Local deterministic replay produced:

  source interaction surfaces            41,412

  axis counterfactual challenges      110,753
  evidence-ablation challenges         41,412
                                    ─────────
  structural adversarial challenges   152,165

  minimum challenges per source probe         3


The counterfactual system changes one represented factor at a time while holding the others fixed. It attacks relationship, state,
context, response channel, and dynamic operator scope.
Examples conceptually:

  Rayan → stranger

  calm → angry

  private → public

  praise → criticism

  success → failure

  verbal → nonverbal

  escalation → de-escalation

  habituation → sensitization


But the alternative registry explicitly says these are adversarial constructions, not claims that those values are true semantic
opposites.
And the challenger registry is:

  challenger_registry_is_ceiling=false

  open_world_challenger_expansion_allowed=true


So later MC7/MC10 implementations can add challenger types we have not conceived yet.

I specifically prevented MC7 from repeating the old “every empty cell is a gap” mistake
This was important.
The old architecture already warned us that an unobserved combination cannot automatically become a personality gap or an
opposite trait. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
Every MC6 surface produces at least one adversarial failure signal because evidence is sparse. But MC7 does not turn 41,412
failures into 41,412 personality traits.
It clusters them.

This directly attacks one of the weaknesses we identified earlier: behavior may depend on what just happened and
accumulated temporal/relationship history, not merely the instantaneous trait/state/context tuple.
The 54 assurance-only candidates are different. They are evidence-fragility issues. They go mainly to MC8/MC9 validation and
do not automatically become MC10 personality-completion targets.
That distinction matters a lot.

 Set-Location C:\A.L.I.C.E-main

 & {
       $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

       $Script = Join-Path `
           $HOME `
           "Downloads\Build-ALICEMC7AtoHCoordinatedAdversarialGapDiscoveryV1-v1.0.0.ps1"

       $ExpectedHash = `
           "EEF69AFDB48E64266BC859F87EB82474BC10A20268825F16F092DACE02CDDEF6"

       if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Script -PathType Leaf)) {
           throw "Missing MC7A-H script: $Script"
       }

       $ActualHash = (
           Get-FileHash `
               -LiteralPath $Script `
               -Algorithm SHA256
       ).Hash

       if ($ActualHash -ne $ExpectedHash) {
           throw "SHA-256 mismatch. Expected=$ExpectedHash Actual=$ActualHash"
       }

       Write-Host "Script verification passed."

       $Tokens = $null
       $ParseErrors = $null

       [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile(
           $Script,
           [ref]$Tokens,
           [ref]$ParseErrors
       ) | Out-Null

       if (@($ParseErrors).Count -gt 0) {
           $ParseErrors | Format-List Message, Extent
           throw "Parser check failed. Script was NOT executed."
       }

       Write-Host "Parser check passed."

       Unblock-File -LiteralPath $Script

       & powershell.exe `
           -NoProfile `
           -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
           -File $Script `
           -RepoRoot "C:\A.L.I.C.E-main" `
           -VaultRoot "C:\ALICE_Vault" `
           -ExpectedHead "0abaed85873c3f8de04765847eb7700b0e20433f"

       if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
           throw "MC7A-H adversarial gap discovery failed with exit code $LASTEXITCODE."

MC8 - Held-out real-E0 reconstruction
I spent extra time on this because MC8 has an easy architectural trap.
MC8 comes before MC9 synthetic-eligibility adjudication and MC10 completion. The original 12-gate architecture places held-out
E0 reconstruction here, before provenance/reality gating and recursive completion. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
Therefore MC8 cannot honestly mean:

  “The finished Alice personality model must already reconstruct all hidden Elaina behavior.”

That model does not exist yet.
Instead MC8 needs to establish a leakage-safe known-answer benchmark, run the strongest E0-only reconstruction baseline
we can run now, expose where that baseline succeeds or fails, and freeze the benchmark so MC9/MC10 cannot cheat later.
That matches the already-ratified hardening rule: known Elaina evidence must be hidden at the complete evidence-
family/leakage-cluster level; default evaluation is grouped five-fold; and the hidden material must be inaccessible to the
generator, retriever, ranker, and prompt builder until after the candidate is frozen. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf The
earlier leakage architecture likewise says random unit splitting is invalid because related Elaina records could leak across
train/test; the strong test is to hide an entire real evidence family and ask whether behavior can be reconstructed from the
remaining E0. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf

I found and resolved one subtle upstream ambiguity before building MC8
The existing leakage map says:

 split_unit = leakage_cluster
 fold_count = 5
 five_fold_assignments_are_deterministic = true
 fold_assignment_hash_basis =
     "leakage_cluster_id SHA-256 modulo 5"



                                       🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                       124 / 172
The persisted registry already has 108 leakage clusters assigned to folds 0..4 . The architecture explicitly requires five
deterministic cluster-level folds and forbids random semantic-unit splitting. ALICE_MIND_COVERAGE_AND_LEAKAGE_MAP_V1.md
But the historical wording does not specify how the SHA-256 digest is converted to an integer.
I tested the actual 108 persisted assignments rather than guessing.
The exact implementation that reproduces them is:

  int(
      sha256(cluster_id.encode()).hexdigest()[:8],
      16
  ) % 5


In other words, the first 32 digest bits are interpreted as an integer and reduced modulo 5.
Result:

  leakage clusters tested        108
  persisted fold matches         108
  mismatches                       0
  fold IDs                   0,1,2,3,4


So I did not rewrite the historical leakage package. MC8 publishes a successor precision binding saying, effectively:

  historical wording:
      SHA-256 modulo 5

  exact operational interpretation:
      first 32 SHA-256 bits → integer → modulo 5


That eliminates ambiguity before MC10 ever consumes these folds.

runtime free-form behavior tests, and held-out adversarial evaluation. It explicitly says Stage G cannot close until a later
executable runtime/model evaluation passes. STAGE_G_IDENTITY_PROVENANCE_BLOCKER_v1.json

Important MC9 eligibility correction
I found a subtle issue that would have mattered badly in MC10 if we did not address it now.
Suppose a graph has four independent E0 evidence families overall. That does not prove those same four families are
relevant to one particular unresolved behavioral interaction.
So MC9 does not say:




                                         🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                        138 / 172
 graph has 4 families
 → A-SYN generation authorized


That would be too loose.
Instead MC9 only grants a pre-generation policy admission.
For the high-impact G1/G2 completion frontier:

 current graph E0 families >= 4
 → admit MC10 packet construction
 → E-INF + A-SYN policy-admissible
 → NOT generation-authorized yet

 current graph E0 families == 3
 → admit MC10 packet construction
 → E-INF policy-admissible
 → A-SYN held pending relevance freeze

 current graph E0 families 1-2
 → HOLD_INSUFFICIENT_INDEPENDENT_E0_FAMILIES

 current graph E0 families == 0
 → HOLD_NO_CURRENT_DIRECT_E0_ANCHOR


Global dynamic gaps and open-world representation gaps get their own discovery/structural dispositions rather than being
treated like ordinary personality synthesis.
Then inside MC10, after the candidate-specific E0 packet has been frozen, the evidence is counted again.
The actual high-impact floors remain:

 E-INF:
 >= 3 candidate-relevant independent E0 families

 A-SYN:
 >= 4 candidate-relevant independent E0 families


Those are the ratified high-impact thresholds. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
This means MC9's output cannot be misread as:

  “105 traits are safe to generate.”

Instead it means:

  “These surfaces may enter the MC10 packet-building process. MC10 must freeze the evidence specific to that exact gap and
  re-prove eligibility before creating even one candidate.”

This is important because the accepted autonomous algorithm explicitly requires evidence to be frozen before generation so
the generator cannot later search for more convenient supporting material. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
I am intentionally not giving you an expected numeric E-INF/A-SYN admission count yet. The script will calculate those
against the exact current Vault state. I don't want a locally reconstructed expectation to become an artificial target that the
runtime somehow gets forced to match.

Static MC9 does not activate generation

 policy admission                        possible

would have caused another failure later.

           $ParseErrors | Format-List Message, Extent
           throw "Parser check failed. Script was NOT executed."
       }

       Write-Host "Parser check passed."

       Unblock-File -LiteralPath $Script

Yes. I did the final cross-document audit against four separate authorities before allowing MC10 to begin:

1. the successful MC9 machine run you just supplied;
2. the last-chat Memory Shadow Migration G record;
3. the capability-first master handoff;
4. the current main README and identity/migration documentation from the A.L.I.C.E. repository.

My conclusion is: the architecture is still on track, the kind of A.L.I.C.E. we promised has not drifted, MC9 is
legitimately closed, and MC10 can begin. There is one documentation sequencing ambiguity worth recording, but it does not
block MC10.

The model we promised vs. what we are actually building
The README's promise is not “train one LLM to be Alice.” It explicitly says A.L.I.C.E. is not one model, that models are
replaceable reasoning engines, and that continuity belongs to the larger cognitive system. It also describes a continuing identity
that can use local, external, specialist, coding, formal, and future models without becoming a different intelligence. (GitHub) The
README further states that no provider, model, checkpoint, or interface owns A.L.I.C.E.'s identity, while the destination includes
owner/source/self models, learning, training, and automatic model adaptation. (GitHub)


                                          🚀 Powered by ChatGPT Exporter                                                                   150 / 172
The current technical identity architecture is even more specific. It requires separate learned/cognitive systems: an Elaina
Identity / Personality Model, a separate Memory Formation Model, a Rayan Host/Owner Model, an A.L.I.C.E.-Rayan Relationship
Model, and an A.L.I.C.E. Continuity/Self Model. Ordinary Rayan data is explicitly forbidden from silently changing the core Elaina-
derived personality anchor. (GitHub) The Memory Formation Model proposes structured memory; deterministic authority services
decide what becomes authoritative. It is explicitly not the personality model. (GitHub)
That is exactly the distinction made in the last-chat architecture. The Elaina Identity / Personality Model is supposed to have
learned weights for temperament, values, reasoning style, emotional tendencies, communication, relationship behavior,
preferences, and behavioral patterns, while Elaina's actual biography remains separately inspectable in the memory fabric. In
other words:

  PERSONALITY WEIGHTS
      = how the Elaina-derived cognition tends to behave

  MEMORY / CLAIM / EVIDENCE FABRIC
      = what actually happened and what is actually known


That document explicitly warns against merging the Memory Formation Model with the personality model. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow
Migration G.pdf

The master handoff independently says the same thing at the larger-system level: evidence → claims → owner/source-
person/self/world models → graph/vector/neural projections → reasoning/action/learning/training. It explicitly supports source-
person reconstruction components, adapters, LoRA, continual-learning experiments, later deeper weight updates, distributed
training/inference, model registries, and challenger evaluation. ALICE_CAPABILITY_FIRST_MEMORY_MASTER_HANDOFF_2026-08-05(3).md
It also requires separate owner, source-person, relationship, self, world, social, causal, belief, and prediction models rather than
collapsing cognition into one monolithic model. ALICE_CAPABILITY_FIRST_MEMORY_MASTER_HANDOFF_2026-08-05(3).md
So MC1→MC9 has not been a detour away from building the personality model. It has been constructing the trustworthy
substrate that makes such a model trainable without turning synthetic guesses, Rayan memories, quotes, visual cues, or inferred
history into opaque weight-level “truth.”

Why the current MC1→MC9 workflow matches the promise
The last-chat MC10 architecture explicitly says the completion system should first operate on the Identity Completion Graph,
not repeatedly fine-tune model weights:

  discover
  → infer
  → synthesize
  → validate
  → graph grows
  → discover again


Only after provisional MC10 saturation do we build/train the completion-aware personality model. The reason given is
inspectability: synthetic conclusions remain provenance-addressable, reversible, challengeable, and removable instead of
disappearing prematurely into weights. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
That is exactly where we are now.

  MC1    ontology breadth                      CLOSED
  MC2    all E0 re-audit                       CLOSED
  MC3    facet adjudication                    CLOSED
  MC4    context/state/role/channel            CLOSED
  MC5    dynamics                              CLOSED
  MC6    3-way / 4-way interactions           CLOSED
  MC7    adversarial gap discovery             CLOSED
  MC8    hidden real-E0 reconstruction         CLOSED
  MC9    provenance/reality/eligibility        CLOSED
                                                     │

  MC10   autonomous completion graph           START

research ambition. ALICE_CAPABILITY_FIRST_MEMORY_MASTER_HANDOFF_2026-08-05(3).md


The one documentation precision debt
There is one wording issue I would not hide.
The public technical document currently says in G2:

  train/evaluate the Elaina Identity / Personality Model from canonical Elaina data

before later Stage-G qualification. (GitHub)
The newer owner-ratified MC10 doctrine is more precise: MC10 first grows the completion graph; the completion-aware
actual personality model is built after provisional saturation. ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf
Those are reconcilable if we distinguish:

  BEFORE MC10 SATURATION
  E0-only identity-model baselines
  research models
  challenger models
  shadow experiments
          = allowed

  AFTER MC10 PROVISIONAL SATURATION
  completion-aware personality model
  using adjudicated E0 + permitted completion material
          = actual intended personality-training stage


The master handoff explicitly allows research, dataset construction, training, shadow inference, and challengers once
lineage/evaluation infrastructure exists, while reserving production influence for measured profiles and owner authority.
ALICE_CAPABILITY_FIRST_MEMORY_MASTER_HANDOFF_2026-08-05(3).md

So this is a future documentation clarification, not an architectural contradiction and not an MC10 blocker. I would
leave the repo untouched during the current Vault qualification work and fix that wording in the later documentation
synchronization PR.

Starting MC10
I am not packaging all of MC10 into one giant run like MC3/MC5.
That would conflict with the design.
MC10 is intentionally recursive. It requires independent discovery rounds, frozen evidence, competing generation,
blinded/challenger evaluation, challenge debt, recursion, and eventual saturation. Synthetic material is forbidden from making
itself look complete. The hardening doctrine requires dual E0/A-SELF frontiers, leakage-isolated pseudo-gap calibration,
generator/judge separation, challenge debt, contextual-variance preservation, off-target tests, and independent final certification.
ChatGPT-Memory Shadow Migration G.pdf

So we begin with MC10A only:

  MC10A

  CLUSTER handoff
         ↓
  CLASSIFY

This implements the first four MC10 operations from the ratified 14-operation pipeline before generation begins. ChatGPT-Memory
Shadow Migration G.pdf

MC10A consumes all 431 MC9 completion clusters. The known structural expansion is:

  399 graph-bound completion candidates

  18 global dynamic candidates
  × 449 high-impact graphs
  = 8,082 concrete dynamic discovery targets

  14 representation-expansion candidates

  399 + 8,082 + 14
  = 8,495 initial MC10 discovery surfaces


The 14 representation candidates remain structural/discovery objects rather than personality truth. The 18 global-dynamic
candidates must first become graph-specific questions.
For each of the 399 graph-bound candidates, MC10A freezes an exact evidence packet containing the candidate-relevant E0
references, source-person authority, evidence-family lineage, semantic authority, source hashes, and cryptographic row hashes.
MC10B may dereference those packets only while their bound sources still match.
And this is deliberately stricter than MC9:

  MC9:
  graph-level evidence looked sufficient
          ↓

  MC10A:
  freeze evidence relevant to THIS EXACT GAP
          ↓
  recount independent E0 families
          ↓
  eligibility may stay the same OR shrink

  eligibility may NOT broaden merely because MC9 admitted it


Therefore do not expect the final MC10A E-INF count to equal 75 or A-SYN count to equal 27. Those are upper bounds
from MC9's pre-freeze policy screen. The real Vault run decides how many survive candidate-specific relevance checking.
MC10A also encodes the cross-document architecture we just audited:

  MC10 recursive updates target Identity Completion Graph first = true
  pre-saturation model research may be shadow-only = true

Everything before tool materialization passed: owner token, repo baseline, MC10A hashes, autonomous-completion architecture,

The failure is a one-character typo in my v1.0.0 expected builder SHA.
Wrong 63-character literal in the launcher:

  82F06CDF2CDB4441DF23265A2F7E93A0B837C02F7C58CCDD2DEDF97A01542E8


Actual embedded builder SHA:

  82F06CDF2CDB4441DF23265A2F7E93A0B837C02F7C58CCDD2DEDDF97A01542E8


The missing character is the extra D around:

  wrong:   ...CCDD2DEDF97...
  correct: ...CCDD2DEDDF97...


That is why PowerShell printed values that looked nearly identical but still correctly rejected them.
